Updates `pyjwt` from 2.13.0 to 2.15.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/releases">pyjwt's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>2.15.0</h2>
<p>See the <a href="https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/blob/2.15.0/CHANGELOG.rst">2.15.0 changelog</a> for complete release details.</p>
<h2>2.14.0</h2>
<p>See the <a href="https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/blob/2.14.0/CHANGELOG.rst">2.14.0 changelog</a> for the complete release details and related security advisories.</p>
</blockquote>
</details>
<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/blob/master/CHANGELOG.rst">pyjwt's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2><code>v2.15.0 &lt;https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/compare/2.14.0...2.15.0&gt;</code>__</h2>
<p>Security</p>
<pre><code>
- Wrap recursion errors from deeply nested JWT payloads in ``DecodeError``
  instead of exposing a raw ``RecursionError``.
<p>Added</p>
<pre><code>
- Support Python 3.15 by @kytta in `[#1202](https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/issues/1202) &amp;lt;https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/pull/1202&amp;gt;`__

Changed
</code></pre>
<ul>
<li><code>JWKSetCache</code> now stores the parsed <code>PyJWKSet</code> rather than the raw JWKS
payload, so a cache hit no longer re-parses every key. <code>JWKSetCache.put()</code>
accepts either form and raises <code>PyJWKSetError</code> for anything else. As a
result, <code>PyJWKClient.get_jwk_set()</code> returns the same <code>PyJWKSet</code> instance
for as long as it stays cached, rather than a freshly built one per call in
<code>[#1208](https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/issues/1208) &amp;lt;https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/pull/1208&amp;gt;</code>__</li>
<li><code>PyJWKClient.fetch_data()</code> now raises
<code>PyJWKClientError(&amp;quot;The JWKS endpoint did not return a JSON object&amp;quot;)</code> when
the endpoint response is not a JSON object, instead of returning it for
<code>get_jwk_set()</code> to reject. Callers reaching the JWKS through
<code>get_jwk_set()</code> see the same error as before in
<code>[#1208](https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/issues/1208) &amp;lt;https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/pull/1208&amp;gt;</code>__</li>
</ul>
<p>Fixed</p>
<pre><code>
- Return cached ``PyJWKSet`` values from ``PyJWKClient.get_jwk_set()`` instead
  of raising ``PyJWKClientError(&amp;quot;The JWKS endpoint did not return a JSON
  object&amp;quot;)``. ``JWKSetCache.put()`` documents ``PyJWKSet`` as the cached value,
  so callers pre-populating the cache to avoid a network round-trip could not
  read it back in `[#914](https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/issues/914) &amp;lt;https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/issues/914&amp;gt;`__ and
  `[#1208](https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/issues/1208) &amp;lt;https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/pull/1208&amp;gt;`__
- ``PyJWKClient.get_jwk_set()`` now caches the key set it returns, so a
  ``fetch_data()`` override that filters or transforms the JWKS is no longer
  undone by the next cache hit in
  `[#1208](https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/issues/1208) &amp;lt;https://github.com/jpadilla/pyjwt/pull/1208&amp;gt;`__
- Raise the documented ``PyJWTError`` subclass instead of leaking a
  ``TypeError`` when the ``exp``, ``nbf``, or ``iat`` claim decodes to a
  non-numeric, non-string value such as a list, dict, or ``null``.
- Reject OKP JWK private keys when their public ``x`` component does not
  match the private ``d`` component.
- Treat malformed JWK Set members as unusable keys rather than letting
  ``AttributeError`` or ``TypeError`` escape ``PyJWKSet``. A member that is not
&amp;lt;/tr&amp;gt;&amp;lt;/table&amp;gt; 
&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt;
&lt;p&gt;... (truncated)&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/details&gt;

Updates `urllib3` from 2.7.0 to 2.8.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/releases">urllib3's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>2.8.0</h2>
<h2>🚀 urllib3 is fundraising for HTTP/2 support</h2>
<p><a href="https://sethmlarson.dev/urllib3-is-fundraising-for-http2-support">urllib3 is raising ~$40,000 USD</a> to release HTTP/2 support and ensure long-term sustainable maintenance of the project. If your company or organization uses Python and would benefit from HTTP/2 support in Requests, pip, cloud SDKs, and thousands of other projects <a href="https://opencollective.com/urllib3">please consider contributing financially</a> to ensure HTTP/2 support is developed sustainably and maintained for the long-haul.</p>
<p>Thank you for your support.</p>
<h2>Security</h2>
<p>Fixed the following security issues:</p>
<ul>
<li>The TLS configuration for HTTPS proxies could be ignored or overridden. (High severity, GHSA-8988-9cw3-xx77)</li>
<li><code>HTTPResponse.stream()</code> and <code>read_chunked()</code> could buffer a chunk-size line of unbounded length in memory. (High severity, GHSA-vxq7-64xx-v4gw)</li>
<li>Chunked Deflate streaming could enter an infinite loop. (Medium severity, GHSA-gh4c-6fx4-qh6g)</li>
</ul>
<blockquote>
<p>[!IMPORTANT]
urllib3 2.8.0 fixes HTTPS proxy TLS configuration being ignored or overridden by destination settings. Configurations relying on that behavior may require changes.</p>
<p>Configure proxy CA certificates and client certificates in <code>proxy_ssl_context</code>, and proxy identity checks with <code>proxy_assert_hostname</code> or <code>proxy_assert_fingerprint</code>. Destination client certificates and identity overrides no longer apply to HTTPS forwarding proxy connections.</p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p>[!NOTE]
CVE IDs had not yet been assigned to these advisories at the time of release due to a backlog at GitHub's CNA.</p>
</blockquote>
<h2>Deprecations &amp; Removals</h2>
<ul>
<li>Deprecated using an empty collection as the <code>Retry</code> option <code>allowed_methods</code> to retry any verb. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5044">#5044</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>Features</h2>
<ul>
<li>Added <code>Url.auth_decoded</code> and <code>Url.auth_decoded_joined</code> convenience properties to the result of <code>parse_url()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/4945">#4945</a>)</li>
<li>Added <code>basic_auth_encoding</code> and <code>proxy_basic_auth_encoding</code> parameters to <code>urllib3.util.make_headers()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5092">#5092</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>Bugfixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed response header handling to replace obsolete folded header lines (<code>obs-fold</code>) with spaces in accordance with RFC 9112, preventing raw CRLF sequences from appearing in header values such as <code>Set-Cookie</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/1362">#1362</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed usage of <code>proxy_ssl_context</code> with <code>ProxyManager</code> when <code>use_forwarding_for_https=True</code>. Passing <code>ssl_context</code> instead of <code>proxy_ssl_context</code> for HTTPS proxies in this configuration now emits a <code>FutureWarning</code> and will raise an error in v3.0. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/2577">#2577</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Changed behavior of the default <code>ConnectionPool.pool</code> initialization. <code>LifoQueue</code> is now resolved from the <code>queue</code> module after the <code>ConnectionPool</code> is instantiated instead of using the default cached <code>QueueCls</code> class property. This is done because sometimes the <code>queue.LifoQueue</code> is monkey-patched late in the program, such as by gevent. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3289">#3289</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Raised <code>UnrewindableBodyError</code> instead of <code>ValueError</code> when retrying a request whose body had <code>tell()</code> but not <code>seek()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3779">#3779</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Decoded percent-encoded SOCKS proxy credentials before authenticating with the proxy server. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3785">#3785</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPResponse.drain_conn()</code> to discard unread response data in 64 KiB chunks (same as the default <code>amt</code> when doing <code>HTTPResponse.stream(...)</code>). (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5019">#5019</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>is_ipaddress()</code> to detect non-standard IPv4 forms accepted by <code>socket.connect</code>, such as hex (<code>0x7f000001</code>), octal (<code>0177.0.0.1</code>), and decimal integers (<code>2130706433</code>), ensuring SSL certificate verification uses the correct mode for these addresses. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5029">#5029</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPConnectionPool.urlopen</code> raising a misleading <code>FullPoolError</code> instead of <code>ValueError</code> when called with an invalid <code>timeout</code> argument on a pool created with <code>block=True</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5059">#5059</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed port-zero handling to preserve explicit <code>:0</code> values instead of substituting the default ports 80 or 443 in URL parsing, pool selection, proxy configuration, <code>connection_from_url()</code>, and HTTP/2 request authority. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5071">#5071</a>, <a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5101">#5101</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a bug where <code>PoolManager</code> passed the <code>assert_hostname</code> and <code>assert_fingerprint</code> parameters to HTTP connection pools. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5077">#5077</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPConnectionPool.urlopen()</code> and HTTP proxy forwarding to strip URL fragments from absolute request targets before sending requests. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5079">#5079</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Added safeguards to the proxy tunneling code to prevent potential security issues when handling invalid characters in the proxy host and HTTP headers. This change affects users of Python 3.10, Python 3.11, and Python 3.12 when the standard library does not contain the fix; those on newer Python versions should upgrade to 3.13.14+ or 3.14.5+ to get the same security fixes. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5091">#5091</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPSConnection.connect()</code> overriding <code>ProxyConfig.ssl_context</code>'s certificate policy and proxy identity checks with the target connection's TLS settings when forwarding through an HTTPS proxy.</p>
<p><code>HTTPSConnection</code> no longer applies target SNI, assertions, or client credentials to forwarding proxy handshakes and continues to use its <code>ssl_context</code> as a fallback when an HTTPS proxy forwards an HTTP target. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5093">#5093</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed URL parsing to more strictly enforce RFC 3986 host syntax, rejecting invalid host input such as raw spaces and control characters, malformed percent-encodings, and percent-encoded control characters in HTTP(S) hosts and IPv6 zone identifiers, including proxy CONNECT tunnel targets. Host normalization now also follows RFC 3986 normalization rules for percent-encoded octets by decoding percent-encoded unreserved characters and uppercasing the hexadecimal digits of retained percent-encoded octets. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5095">#5095</a>)</p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>

Updates `urllib3` from 2.7.0 to 2.8.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/releases">urllib3's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>2.8.0</h2>
<h2>🚀 urllib3 is fundraising for HTTP/2 support</h2>
<p><a href="https://sethmlarson.dev/urllib3-is-fundraising-for-http2-support">urllib3 is raising ~$40,000 USD</a> to release HTTP/2 support and ensure long-term sustainable maintenance of the project. If your company or organization uses Python and would benefit from HTTP/2 support in Requests, pip, cloud SDKs, and thousands of other projects <a href="https://opencollective.com/urllib3">please consider contributing financially</a> to ensure HTTP/2 support is developed sustainably and maintained for the long-haul.</p>
<p>Thank you for your support.</p>
<h2>Security</h2>
<p>Fixed the following security issues:</p>
<ul>
<li>The TLS configuration for HTTPS proxies could be ignored or overridden. (High severity, GHSA-8988-9cw3-xx77)</li>
<li><code>HTTPResponse.stream()</code> and <code>read_chunked()</code> could buffer a chunk-size line of unbounded length in memory. (High severity, GHSA-vxq7-64xx-v4gw)</li>
<li>Chunked Deflate streaming could enter an infinite loop. (Medium severity, GHSA-gh4c-6fx4-qh6g)</li>
</ul>
<blockquote>
<p>[!IMPORTANT]
urllib3 2.8.0 fixes HTTPS proxy TLS configuration being ignored or overridden by destination settings. Configurations relying on that behavior may require changes.</p>
<p>Configure proxy CA certificates and client certificates in <code>proxy_ssl_context</code>, and proxy identity checks with <code>proxy_assert_hostname</code> or <code>proxy_assert_fingerprint</code>. Destination client certificates and identity overrides no longer apply to HTTPS forwarding proxy connections.</p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p>[!NOTE]
CVE IDs had not yet been assigned to these advisories at the time of release due to a backlog at GitHub's CNA.</p>
</blockquote>
<h2>Deprecations &amp; Removals</h2>
<ul>
<li>Deprecated using an empty collection as the <code>Retry</code> option <code>allowed_methods</code> to retry any verb. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5044">#5044</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>Features</h2>
<ul>
<li>Added <code>Url.auth_decoded</code> and <code>Url.auth_decoded_joined</code> convenience properties to the result of <code>parse_url()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/4945">#4945</a>)</li>
<li>Added <code>basic_auth_encoding</code> and <code>proxy_basic_auth_encoding</code> parameters to <code>urllib3.util.make_headers()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5092">#5092</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>Bugfixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed response header handling to replace obsolete folded header lines (<code>obs-fold</code>) with spaces in accordance with RFC 9112, preventing raw CRLF sequences from appearing in header values such as <code>Set-Cookie</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/1362">#1362</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed usage of <code>proxy_ssl_context</code> with <code>ProxyManager</code> when <code>use_forwarding_for_https=True</code>. Passing <code>ssl_context</code> instead of <code>proxy_ssl_context</code> for HTTPS proxies in this configuration now emits a <code>FutureWarning</code> and will raise an error in v3.0. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/2577">#2577</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Changed behavior of the default <code>ConnectionPool.pool</code> initialization. <code>LifoQueue</code> is now resolved from the <code>queue</code> module after the <code>ConnectionPool</code> is instantiated instead of using the default cached <code>QueueCls</code> class property. This is done because sometimes the <code>queue.LifoQueue</code> is monkey-patched late in the program, such as by gevent. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3289">#3289</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Raised <code>UnrewindableBodyError</code> instead of <code>ValueError</code> when retrying a request whose body had <code>tell()</code> but not <code>seek()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3779">#3779</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Decoded percent-encoded SOCKS proxy credentials before authenticating with the proxy server. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3785">#3785</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPResponse.drain_conn()</code> to discard unread response data in 64 KiB chunks (same as the default <code>amt</code> when doing <code>HTTPResponse.stream(...)</code>). (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5019">#5019</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>is_ipaddress()</code> to detect non-standard IPv4 forms accepted by <code>socket.connect</code>, such as hex (<code>0x7f000001</code>), octal (<code>0177.0.0.1</code>), and decimal integers (<code>2130706433</code>), ensuring SSL certificate verification uses the correct mode for these addresses. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5029">#5029</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPConnectionPool.urlopen</code> raising a misleading <code>FullPoolError</code> instead of <code>ValueError</code> when called with an invalid <code>timeout</code> argument on a pool created with <code>block=True</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5059">#5059</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed port-zero handling to preserve explicit <code>:0</code> values instead of substituting the default ports 80 or 443 in URL parsing, pool selection, proxy configuration, <code>connection_from_url()</code>, and HTTP/2 request authority. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5071">#5071</a>, <a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5101">#5101</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a bug where <code>PoolManager</code> passed the <code>assert_hostname</code> and <code>assert_fingerprint</code> parameters to HTTP connection pools. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5077">#5077</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPConnectionPool.urlopen()</code> and HTTP proxy forwarding to strip URL fragments from absolute request targets before sending requests. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5079">#5079</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Added safeguards to the proxy tunneling code to prevent potential security issues when handling invalid characters in the proxy host and HTTP headers. This change affects users of Python 3.10, Python 3.11, and Python 3.12 when the standard library does not contain the fix; those on newer Python versions should upgrade to 3.13.14+ or 3.14.5+ to get the same security fixes. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5091">#5091</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPSConnection.connect()</code> overriding <code>ProxyConfig.ssl_context</code>'s certificate policy and proxy identity checks with the target connection's TLS settings when forwarding through an HTTPS proxy.</p>
<p><code>HTTPSConnection</code> no longer applies target SNI, assertions, or client credentials to forwarding proxy handshakes and continues to use its <code>ssl_context</code> as a fallback when an HTTPS proxy forwards an HTTP target. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5093">#5093</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed URL parsing to more strictly enforce RFC 3986 host syntax, rejecting invalid host input such as raw spaces and control characters, malformed percent-encodings, and percent-encoded control characters in HTTP(S) hosts and IPv6 zone identifiers, including proxy CONNECT tunnel targets. Host normalization now also follows RFC 3986 normalization rules for percent-encoded octets by decoding percent-encoded unreserved characters and uppercasing the hexadecimal digits of retained percent-encoded octets. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5095">#5095</a>)</p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>

<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/blob/main/CHANGES.rst">urllib3's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h1>2.8.0 (2026-09-15)</h1>
<h2>Security</h2>
<p>Fixed the following security issues:</p>
<ul>
<li>The TLS configuration for HTTPS proxies could be ignored or overridden.
(High severity, <code>GHSA-8988-9cw3-xx77 &lt;https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/security/advisories/GHSA-8988-9cw3-xx77&gt;</code>__)</li>
<li><code>HTTPResponse.stream()</code> and <code>read_chunked()</code> could buffer a chunk-size
line of unbounded length in memory. (High severity,
<code>GHSA-vxq7-64xx-v4gw &lt;https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/security/advisories/GHSA-vxq7-64xx-v4gw&gt;</code>__)</li>
<li>Chunked Deflate streaming could enter an infinite loop. (Medium severity,
<code>GHSA-gh4c-6fx4-qh6g &lt;https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/security/advisories/GHSA-gh4c-6fx4-qh6g&gt;</code>__)</li>
</ul>
<p>.. caution::</p>
<pre><code>urllib3 2.8.0 fixes HTTPS proxy TLS configuration being ignored or
overridden by destination settings. Configurations relying on that
behavior may require changes.
<p>Configure proxy CA certificates and client certificates in
<code>proxy_ssl_context</code>, and proxy identity checks with
<code>proxy_assert_hostname</code> or <code>proxy_assert_fingerprint</code>.
Destination client certificates and identity overrides no longer
apply to HTTPS forwarding proxy connections.
</code></pre></p>
<h2>Deprecations &amp; Removals</h2>
<ul>
<li>Deprecated using an empty collection as the <code>Retry</code> option
<code>allowed_methods</code> to retry any verb.
(<code>[#5044](https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5044) &lt;https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5044&gt;</code>__)</li>
</ul>
<h2>Features</h2>
<ul>
<li>Added <code>Url.auth_decoded</code> and <code>Url.auth_decoded_joined</code> convenience
properties to the result of <code>parse_url()</code>.
(<code>[#4945](https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/4945) &lt;https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/4945&gt;</code>__)</li>
<li>Added <code>basic_auth_encoding</code> and <code>proxy_basic_auth_encoding</code> parameters to
<code>urllib3.util.make_headers()</code>.
(<code>[#5092](https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5092) &lt;https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5092&gt;</code>__)</li>
</ul>
<h2>Bugfixes</h2>

<blockquote>
<h2>v1.88.6</h2>
<h2>Verify Docker Image Signature</h2>
<p>All LiteLLM Docker images are signed with <a href="https://docs.sigstore.dev/cosign/overview/">cosign</a>. Every release is signed with the same key introduced in <a href="https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/commit/0112e53046018d726492c814b3644b7d376029d0">commit <code>0112e53</code></a>.</p>
<p><strong>Verify using the pinned commit hash (recommended):</strong></p>
<p>A commit hash is cryptographically immutable, so this is the strongest way to ensure you are using the original signing key:</p>
<pre lang="bash"><code>cosign verify \
  --key https://raw.githubusercontent.com/BerriAI/litellm/0112e53046018d726492c814b3644b7d376029d0/cosign.pub \
  ghcr.io/berriai/litellm:v1.88.6
</code></pre>
<p><strong>Verify using the release tag (convenience):</strong></p>
<p>Tags are protected in this repository and resolve to the same key. This option is easier to read but relies on tag protection rules:</p>
<pre lang="bash"><code>cosign verify \
  --key https://raw.githubusercontent.com/BerriAI/litellm/v1.88.6/cosign.pub \
  ghcr.io/berriai/litellm:v1.88.6
</code></pre>
<p>Expected output:</p>
<pre><code>The following checks were performed on each of these signatures:
  - The cosign claims were validated
  - The signatures were verified against the specified public key
</code></pre>
<hr />

</blockquote>

Updates `urllib3` from 2.7.0 to 2.8.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/urllib3/urllib3/releases">urllib3's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>2.8.0</h2>
<h2>🚀 urllib3 is fundraising for HTTP/2 support</h2>
<p><a href="https://sethmlarson.dev/urllib3-is-fundraising-for-http2-support">urllib3 is raising ~$40,000 USD</a> to release HTTP/2 support and ensure long-term sustainable maintenance of the project. If your company or organization uses Python and would benefit from HTTP/2 support in Requests, pip, cloud SDKs, and thousands of other projects <a href="https://opencollective.com/urllib3">please consider contributing financially</a> to ensure HTTP/2 support is developed sustainably and maintained for the long-haul.</p>
<p>Thank you for your support.</p>
<h2>Security</h2>
<p>Fixed the following security issues:</p>
<ul>
<li>The TLS configuration for HTTPS proxies could be ignored or overridden. (High severity, GHSA-8988-9cw3-xx77)</li>
<li><code>HTTPResponse.stream()</code> and <code>read_chunked()</code> could buffer a chunk-size line of unbounded length in memory. (High severity, GHSA-vxq7-64xx-v4gw)</li>
<li>Chunked Deflate streaming could enter an infinite loop. (Medium severity, GHSA-gh4c-6fx4-qh6g)</li>
</ul>
<blockquote>
<p>[!IMPORTANT]
urllib3 2.8.0 fixes HTTPS proxy TLS configuration being ignored or overridden by destination settings. Configurations relying on that behavior may require changes.</p>
<p>Configure proxy CA certificates and client certificates in <code>proxy_ssl_context</code>, and proxy identity checks with <code>proxy_assert_hostname</code> or <code>proxy_assert_fingerprint</code>. Destination client certificates and identity overrides no longer apply to HTTPS forwarding proxy connections.</p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p>[!NOTE]
CVE IDs had not yet been assigned to these advisories at the time of release due to a backlog at GitHub's CNA.</p>
</blockquote>
<h2>Deprecations &amp; Removals</h2>
<ul>
<li>Deprecated using an empty collection as the <code>Retry</code> option <code>allowed_methods</code> to retry any verb. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5044">#5044</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>Features</h2>
<ul>
<li>Added <code>Url.auth_decoded</code> and <code>Url.auth_decoded_joined</code> convenience properties to the result of <code>parse_url()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/4945">#4945</a>)</li>
<li>Added <code>basic_auth_encoding</code> and <code>proxy_basic_auth_encoding</code> parameters to <code>urllib3.util.make_headers()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5092">#5092</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>Bugfixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed response header handling to replace obsolete folded header lines (<code>obs-fold</code>) with spaces in accordance with RFC 9112, preventing raw CRLF sequences from appearing in header values such as <code>Set-Cookie</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/1362">#1362</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed usage of <code>proxy_ssl_context</code> with <code>ProxyManager</code> when <code>use_forwarding_for_https=True</code>. Passing <code>ssl_context</code> instead of <code>proxy_ssl_context</code> for HTTPS proxies in this configuration now emits a <code>FutureWarning</code> and will raise an error in v3.0. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/2577">#2577</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Changed behavior of the default <code>ConnectionPool.pool</code> initialization. <code>LifoQueue</code> is now resolved from the <code>queue</code> module after the <code>ConnectionPool</code> is instantiated instead of using the default cached <code>QueueCls</code> class property. This is done because sometimes the <code>queue.LifoQueue</code> is monkey-patched late in the program, such as by gevent. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3289">#3289</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Raised <code>UnrewindableBodyError</code> instead of <code>ValueError</code> when retrying a request whose body had <code>tell()</code> but not <code>seek()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3779">#3779</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Decoded percent-encoded SOCKS proxy credentials before authenticating with the proxy server. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/3785">#3785</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPResponse.drain_conn()</code> to discard unread response data in 64 KiB chunks (same as the default <code>amt</code> when doing <code>HTTPResponse.stream(...)</code>). (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5019">#5019</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>is_ipaddress()</code> to detect non-standard IPv4 forms accepted by <code>socket.connect</code>, such as hex (<code>0x7f000001</code>), octal (<code>0177.0.0.1</code>), and decimal integers (<code>2130706433</code>), ensuring SSL certificate verification uses the correct mode for these addresses. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5029">#5029</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPConnectionPool.urlopen</code> raising a misleading <code>FullPoolError</code> instead of <code>ValueError</code> when called with an invalid <code>timeout</code> argument on a pool created with <code>block=True</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5059">#5059</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed port-zero handling to preserve explicit <code>:0</code> values instead of substituting the default ports 80 or 443 in URL parsing, pool selection, proxy configuration, <code>connection_from_url()</code>, and HTTP/2 request authority. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5071">#5071</a>, <a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5101">#5101</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a bug where <code>PoolManager</code> passed the <code>assert_hostname</code> and <code>assert_fingerprint</code> parameters to HTTP connection pools. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5077">#5077</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPConnectionPool.urlopen()</code> and HTTP proxy forwarding to strip URL fragments from absolute request targets before sending requests. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5079">#5079</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Added safeguards to the proxy tunneling code to prevent potential security issues when handling invalid characters in the proxy host and HTTP headers. This change affects users of Python 3.10, Python 3.11, and Python 3.12 when the standard library does not contain the fix; those on newer Python versions should upgrade to 3.13.14+ or 3.14.5+ to get the same security fixes. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5091">#5091</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed <code>HTTPSConnection.connect()</code> overriding <code>ProxyConfig.ssl_context</code>'s certificate policy and proxy identity checks with the target connection's TLS settings when forwarding through an HTTPS proxy.</p>
<p><code>HTTPSConnection</code> no longer applies target SNI, assertions, or client credentials to forwarding proxy handshakes and continues to use its <code>ssl_context</code> as a fallback when an HTTPS proxy forwards an HTTP target. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5093">#5093</a>)</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed URL parsing to more strictly enforce RFC 3986 host syntax, rejecting invalid host input such as raw spaces and control characters, malformed percent-encodings, and percent-encoded control characters in HTTP(S) hosts and IPv6 zone identifiers, including proxy CONNECT tunnel targets. Host normalization now also follows RFC 3986 normalization rules for percent-encoded octets by decoding percent-encoded unreserved characters and uppercasing the hexadecimal digits of retained percent-encoded octets. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/urllib3/urllib3/issues/5095">#5095</a>)</p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>

# Change

Record removal in `drainCutoverHolds` is fenced to the lease owner (`packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/cutover-hold.ts:255-258`). When a drainer settles a turn while a *different* replica owns the lease, the record survives. If that replica dies without draining, the next drainer acquires the lapsed lease, finds a live record, and redelivers a turn whose dedup ledger already reads `delivered`. The Cloud turn and the provider send run a second time.

The drainer now reads the dedup ledger after acquiring the lease and before the expiry check (`src/cutover-hold.ts:281-282`). Only a `held` ledger is redelivered. `holdWebhookForCutover` writes `held` with no TTL, so while a record exists the ledger leaves `held` only when a drainer settles the turn: `delivered` after a completed redelivery, or reopened (absent) after a non-retryable release. Any such value means the turn is settled, so the record is removed under the same owner fence and counted in a new `stale` statistic (`CutoverHoldDrainStats.stale`, `:173`), with an info log naming the key and the ledger value. The ordering matters: the check runs before the hold-budget expiry branch, because `release(held, "expired")` would otherwise reopen a `delivered` ledger for a settled turn.

Risk: low. The only changed path is a record whose ledger is not `held` at drain time; every other outcome is unchanged. `stale` is additive on the stats object; the drain summary in `src/index.ts` now counts it in its non-empty check and logs it with the other counts, and the tests assert it.

Not addressed: two live drainers running the same turn concurrently after a renewal failure. The ledger stays `held` until a turn settles, so this check cannot see that window; lease renewal is the guard for it.

# Testing

Environment: Bun 1.3.14 on this machine (the repository pins 1.4.2), Node 24.15.0, `@elizaos/core` resolved from source through `--conditions=eliza-source` (no `dist` in this checkout). Not CI parity. All runs from `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook`.

New test, `a hold settled while another replica owned the lease is not redelivered once that lease lapses` (`__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts:486`): drives the real `handleWebhook` and `drainCutoverHolds` against the local `Bun.serve` Cloud stub and in-memory Redis. While the attested turn runs, the lease key is overwritten with another replica's token; after the first drain the lease is deleted and a second drain runs. It asserts that the record survives the first drain (fenced), that the second drain reports `stale: 1` and nothing else, one Cloud turn, one provider send, the record removed, and a `delivered` ledger. The six existing `toEqual` stats assertions gain `stale: 0`.

## Known gaps / failures

- Bun 1.3.14 was used locally; the repository pins 1.4.2. The suite resolves `@elizaos/core` from source via `--conditions=eliza-source`. Not CI parity.
- Redis Lua ran on ioredis-mock, not on live Upstash or native Redis.
- `bun run verify` was not run locally.
- The concurrent-drainers window described under Change is out of scope.

## Deployment

None. No migration or configuration change; existing held records are handled by the new check on their next drain.

# Risks

**Low.** The change adds a new optional field to an internal transform record and a strictly-scoped null-drop during tool-call argument restoration in `plugins/plugin-openai`:

- Only the non-Cerebras strict-tool path (`!preserveOptional && !preserveStructure`) registers the new null-strip transforms; Cerebras (`preserveOptional`) and response-schema (`preserveStructure`) behavior is byte-identical (guarded by the existing `cerebras-native-tools-walk-bound.shape.test.ts` exact-transform assertions, all passing).
- Required-at-origin nulls are NOT stripped — they still fail admission visibly (no silent success fabricated). Nullable-at-origin nulls (`type: ["string","null"]`, `type: "null"`, anyOf/oneOf null branches) are preserved as real values.
- Restore is applied only to named properties declared at the transform path; record-entry values, array elements, and undeclared keys are untouched.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Strict non-Cerebras native tool schemas require every property (`sanitizeJsonSchema`'s all-properties-required rule), so lenient OpenAI-compatible providers answer an origin-optional argument with `null`. The reverse mapping (`restoreRecordArgToolCalls`) removed the `__eliza_record_entries` carrier but kept those nulls, and `validateToolArgs` — which correctly re-checks the caller's original schema, where those properties are optional and not nullable — then rejected the entire tool call with errors like:

```
Argument 'operations[0].workThreadId' expected string, got null
Argument 'operations[0].sourceWorkThreadIds' expected array, got null
...
```

This PR records, per all-required schema node, the origin-optional property names whose provider null means "omitted" (`omitNullProperties` on `RecordArgTransform`), and drops exactly those nulls during restore. Admission then judges the restored arguments against the original schema as designed.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

The failing test was introduced in `bcc773485b` (Sept 15) to pin the real provider behavior ("non-Cerebras strict tools also represent omitted optionals as null") and has been red on `develop` since introduction, contributing to the chronically failing **Develop Full** workflow. The modeled payload is not hypothetical: it is the observed live shape of strict OpenAI-compatible endpoints answering sparse multi-operation actions such as `WORK_THREAD`. Left unfixed, every such native tool call fails admission on the non-Cerebras path.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

32:T21a0,# Relates to

#32930 (expire stale sleep decisions in merged schedule state) fixed the **local** merge. The **Cloud** merge of the same device's synced rows can still report the owner asleep after they woke. These were the two blocking points of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32930#pullrequestreview-5353799309). Both are unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`.

**1. Every synced row gets the batch's send time.**

- `refreshEffectiveScheduleState` sends the device's last 48 h of `local_inference` rows in one batch, with `observedAt: now` and no per-row time (`reminders-service.ts:2306-2313`, serializer at `:1997-2023`).
- `recordsFromSyncRequest` stamps that one value on every record (`schedule-state.ts:518-532`), and the upsert keeps it (`schedule-projections.ts:678`).
- In the Cloud merge, an old sleep row therefore has the same `observedAt` as the newer wake row from the same device. It survives the per-device supersession (`schedule-state.ts:659-679`), and the sleep-first tie-break (`STATE_RANK`, `:52`) picks `sleeping`.

**2. A synced state row adopts its snapshot's meal forecast as its label.**

- `recordFromSyncInput` sets `mealLabel: args.input.mealLabel ?? snapshot.nextMealLabel ?? null` (`schedule-state.ts:508`).
- Local state rows have `mealLabel: null`, but their snapshot carries the insight's `nextMealLabel` (for example the morning's `breakfast` forecast).
- In the Cloud those rows become labelled rows. Labelled rows are excluded from the circadian vote (`:665`, `:675`), so a wake row that forecasts breakfast drops out, and the old sleep row decides.

Replaying a device's rows through the Cloud path on develop (sleep at 07:00, wake at 13:30, merged at 14:00):

| Wake row | Local merge | Cloud merge on develop |
| --- | --- | --- |
| no meal forecast | `waking` | **`sleeping`**, `currentSleepStartedAt` 07:00 |
| snapshot forecasts `breakfast` | `waking` | **`sleeping`**, `currentSleepStartedAt` 07:00 |

# Change

- **Contract:** `SyncLifeOpsScheduleObservationInput` (plugin-elizacloud) gains an optional `observedAt`, the time the device recorded that row.
- **Serializer:** it moves into `schedule-state.ts` as `scheduleObservationSyncInput` and includes the row's `observedAt`. `RemindersDomain.serializeScheduleObservationForSync` delegates to it. The tests use the real serializer.
- **Ingest:** `ingestScheduleObservations` normalizes the per-row `observedAt`. Before this change the field would have been dropped silently.
- **`recordsFromSyncRequest`:** each row keeps its own `observedAt`. A missing value, or one after the batch's send time, falls back to the send time (the previous behavior), so a device clock cannot place a row in the future.
- **`recordFromSyncInput`:** a synced row's `mealLabel` is only the label it was sent with. Real meal rows are always sent with an explicit label (`deriveLocalScheduleObservations`), and the snapshot still keeps `nextMealLabel`.

Older senders that omit the per-row time keep the current behavior. An older Cloud agent drops the unknown field during normalization.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`4573712ebf`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

The new tests are in `schedule-state-freshness.test.ts` (vitest). Each one builds the device's stored rows, serializes them with `scheduleObservationSyncInput`, ingests them with `recordsFromSyncRequest` as `device_sync` in one batch sent at `now`, and merges with `scope: "cloud"`:

- *a synced batch keeps each row's own time, so replayed sleep does not beat the newer wake*: `waking`, `currentSleepStartedAt: null`, and the row times are preserved.
- *a synced state row does not adopt its snapshot's meal forecast as its own label*: both rows have `mealLabel: null`, and the merge result is `waking`.
- *an explicit meal row keeps its label through sync*.
- *a row time after the batch send time falls back to the send time*.

**Develop reproduction:** a throwaway test (not committed) serialized rows exactly as develop's `serializeScheduleObservationForSync` does and ran both scenarios against develop's `schedule-state.ts`:

```
REPRO-1 cloud: sleeping 2026-09-29T07:00:00.000Z
REPRO-2 cloud: sleeping 2026-09-29T07:00:00.000Z
   × develop: replayed sleep vs newer wake (no meal forecast)
   × develop: wake row whose snapshot forecasts breakfast
```

## Known gaps / failures

- `bun run verify` was not run in full on this host.
- The fix takes effect once the Cloud-hosted agent runs this version. Until then its normalization drops the per-row time, and the behavior stays as on develop.
- Cloud rows already mislabelled by the snapshot forecast keep their label until they age out of the 48 h window. Their ids hash the label, so the corrected rows are new rows, and the old labelled rows do not vote on the state in the meantime.
- Nothing here drives `ingestScheduleObservations` end to end with a repository. The per-row normalization is the same `normalizeOptionalIsoString` used for `windowEndAt`.

None. No migration or configuration change.

Updates `drizzle-orm` from 0.44.7 to 0.45.3
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/releases">drizzle-orm's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>0.45.3</h2>
<h2>New Netlify DB Driver</h2>
<blockquote>
<p>Note: The Netlify DB driver is developed and maintained by the Netlify team.</p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Installation:</strong></p>
<pre lang="bash"><code>npm i @netlify/db
</code></pre>
<p><strong>Usage example:</strong></p>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';
<p>// reads NETLIFY_DB_URL and NETLIFY_DB_DRIVER env vars
const db = drizzle();</p>
<p>const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre></p>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';

const db = drizzle(process.env.DATABASE_URL);

const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';

// Explicit client — consumer controls the driver
const db = drizzle({ client: netlifyDbClient });

const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre>
<h2>0.45.2</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed <code>sql.identifier()</code>, <code>sql.as()</code> escaping issues. Previously all the values passed to this functions were not properly escaped
causing a possible SQL Injection (CWE-89) vulnerability</li>
</ul>
<p>Thanks to <a href="https://github.com/EthanKim88"><code>@​EthanKim88</code></a>, <a href="https://github.com/0x90sh"><code>@​0x90sh</code></a> and <a href="https://github.com/wgoodall01"><code>@​wgoodall01</code></a> for reaching out to us with a reproduction and suggested fix</p>
<h2>0.45.1</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed pg-native Pool detection in node-postgres transactions breaking in environments with forbidden <code>require()</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/5107">#5107</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>0.45.0</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed pg-native Pool detection in node-postgres transactions</li>
<li>Allowed subqueries in select fields</li>
<li>Updated typo algorythm =&gt; algorithm</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

36:T391e,# Relates to

Closes #32945
Refs #32915

`POST /api/crypto/payments` answered 400 "Amount too large" for `10.005`. #32915 added a whole-cents rejection to `validatePaymentAmount`, and `createPayment` chose its error code by searching the validator's message. Any message without "at least" was classified as too large.

# Change

1. **The validator returns a typed reason.** `validatePaymentAmount` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/config/crypto.ts:229-265`) now returns `{ valid: true }` or `{ valid: false; reason; error }`. `reason` is one of `not_whole_cents`, `below_minimum` or `above_maximum`. The checks, their order and the `error` messages are unchanged. The existing callers only read `valid` and `error`, and they compile unchanged, including `direct-wallet-payments.ts:1468-1469`. That caller throws the message as a plain `Error` and never classified it.
2. **The service maps reasons to codes through a table, not the message.** `AMOUNT_REJECTION_CODES` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/crypto-payments.ts:66-70`) is checked with `satisfies Record<PaymentAmountRejection, CryptoPaymentErrorCode>`, so a new reason will not compile without a code. `not_whole_cents` maps to the new `AMOUNT_INVALID` (`:39`), `below_minimum` to `AMOUNT_TOO_SMALL`, and `above_maximum` to `AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE`. `createPayment` throws with the validator's message (`:329`). The old `|| "Invalid amount"` fallback could never be reached, because every rejection carries a message.
3. **The route maps the new code to 400.** `packages/cloud/api/crypto/payments/route.ts:96-99` adds `AMOUNT_INVALID → 400 "Amount must be a USD value in whole cents"`. Without that entry the code would fall through to the 500 default (`:106-109`).

Consumers checked with `git grep -n "AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE\|AMOUNT_TOO_SMALL\|CryptoPaymentError\|validatePaymentAmount"` across `packages/` and `plugins/`. The route above is the only code that maps these codes. The billing UI (`packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/components/billing-tab.tsx:429`) and the `plugin-elizacloud` billing proxy (`plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/routes/cloud-billing-routes.ts:730-742`) show or forward the `error` text and do not switch on codes, so neither needed a change. The direct-wallet route (`packages/cloud/api/crypto/direct-payments/route.ts:80-84`) returns the validator message as a 400, and that message is unchanged.

Risk: low. Valid, too-small and too-large amounts get the same code, message, HTTP status and response body as before. The tests below assert the exact response bytes. The only change callers see is the response for an amount with more than two decimal places: 400 "Amount must be a USD value in whole cents" instead of 400 "Amount too large". `NaN` and `Infinity` now produce the `AMOUNT_INVALID` code, but they reach `createPayment` only from direct callers, because JSON cannot carry them.

This fixes two replay defects in the durable connector hold that #32909 introduced. I found them while reviewing #32909.

# Contribution provenance

<!-- contribution-attribution:v1 -->
<!-- attribution-row:ai-assistance -->
- AI assistance: `yes`
<!-- attribution-row:models -->

- Agent tooling: `Claude Code`
<!-- attribution-row:skill-revision -->

# Change

Both defects affect only Blooio and Twilio turns that the gateway has already acknowledged to the provider and parked for a Shared→Dedicated cutover.

1. **Non-retryable Cloud refusals are released at once.** `redeliverHeldWebhook` re-held every `PersonalSharedPreEgressError`. That included failures that Cloud classified as `retryable: false`: a 4xx, or `X-Eliza-Retryable: false`, as read by `readPersonalSharedFailureMetadata` (`packages/cloud/services/_common/src/personal-shared-failure.ts:100-102`, `:127-132`). Such a turn was sent to Cloud again on every drain for the one-hour hold budget, and then logged as a cutover expiry. The guard (`src/webhook-handler.ts:954-957`) now keeps a turn held only when Cloud returned a cutover hold, or when the failure is not classified as non-retryable. A non-retryable failure falls through to `settleBackgroundDeliveryFailure` (`:963`), the same path a first background delivery takes. That path logs `Background message processing failed` at error level. Because a pre-egress failure means no provider egress happened, it also reopens the dedup key (`:835-867`). This is the ledger contract that function documents for idempotent pre-egress failures, so a refused redelivery ends in the same state as a refused first delivery. Transport errors, 5xx responses and cutover holds stay in the durable queue, as #32909 intends.
2. **A settled record is no longer kept because its lease lapsed.** `drainCutoverHolds` removed a delivered or released record only while the lease still held this drainer's token. Suppose renewal failed for a whole lease period and no other replica took the lease. The ledger already read `delivered`, but the record and index entry stayed, and the next drain ran the Cloud turn and the provider send again. The removal script (`src/cutover-hold.ts:247-260`) now refuses only when a *different* owner holds the lease. The existing tests `an expired drainer cannot mutate a replacement lease` and `a drainer releases only its own lease` still pass unchanged. The reschedule write (`:139`) keeps its strict token fence. With no lease, a `held` outcome is simply not stored, and the unchanged record is retried on the next drain.

Risk: low. Only two paths change: the redelivery of a failure with `retryable: false`, and record removal when the lease key is absent. Every other outcome is unchanged. That covers cutover holds, transient failures, a lease owned by another replica, and expiry.

Not addressed: a lease taken by another replica that dies without draining (probe P2 in the #32909 review). The first drainer still correctly declines to remove the record, and the record is replayed once that replica's lease lapses. Covering it would mean checking the dedup ledger before redelivery. It needs a live owner to steal the lease, which lease renewal is designed to prevent.

# Testing

Environment: Bun 1.3.14 on this machine (the repository pins 1.4.2), Node 24.15.0. `@elizaos/core` resolves from source through `--conditions=eliza-source` because no `dist` is built in this checkout. This is not CI parity. Prepared on the #32909 head `bc812ab321` as commit `332bb56054`, then rebased as the single commit `190d8a3304` onto `origin/develop` `4573712ebf`, which already contains #32909.

The tests are in `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts`. They drive the real `handleWebhook`, `drainCutoverHolds`, `redeliverHeldWebhook` and `releaseExpiredHeldWebhook` against a local `Bun.serve` Cloud route and the in-memory Redis adapter. Only the Blooio provider API is substituted.

- `a delivered hold whose lease lapsed with no new owner is not replayed`: the lease key is deleted while the attested turn runs. The test asserts 1 Cloud turn, 1 provider send, `delivered: 1` on the first drain, the record removed, all-zero stats on the next drain, and a `delivered` ledger.
- `a held turn Cloud refuses as not retryable is released at once, not replayed for the hold budget`: Cloud answers 400. The test asserts `released: 1`, a reopened (null) ledger, the record removed, all-zero stats on a drain after the hold budget, 1 Cloud turn and 0 provider sends.

1. GREEN at the rebased head `190d8a3304`, from `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook`: `bun test --conditions=eliza-source` → **46 pass, 0 fail**; Biome on the three files and `tsc --noEmit` were rerun there too (both clean). The transcript below was captured at the pre-rebase commit `332bb56054` with the same result: **46 pass, 0 fail, 154 expect() calls, 5 files**. The file alone gives **9 pass, 0 fail, 56 expect() calls**; #32909's head has 7 tests in it.
2. RED, with both source files checked out from `bc812ab321` (the #32909 head) and the new tests kept → **7 pass, 2 fail**. 38:T705,## Summary

Fixes the web dev UI crashing into the generic "Something went wrong" error boundary in `<AppProviderInner>` on any machine with the React DevTools extension installed.

## Root cause

`redactLogValue` (`packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.ts`) cloned log payloads into null-prototype (`Object.create(null)`) targets. Null-prototype objects throw `TypeError: Cannot convert object to primitive value` on `String()`/template coercion. React DevTools patches `console.info` with exactly such a coercion, so the startup logger call in `useStartupCoordinator.ts:242` (`logger.info('[startup-coordinator] phase=...')`) threw on every phase change, tripping the `ErrorBoundary`.

Repro (before fix): `String(redactTrailingArgs([{}])[0])` throws. Only machines *with* the extension were affected, which is why clean headless sessions loaded fine.

## Fix

Clone targets are now plain `{}` objects via a `createRedactClone()` helper. The `__proto__` protection never came from the prototype — it comes from `defineSafeProperty` using `Object.defineProperty`, which creates an own data property without invoking the inherited setter. Behavior of redaction is otherwise unchanged (credential masking, fail-closed markers).

## Validation

- New `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.test.ts` (5 tests): coercion survival for plain/Map/Set clones, `__proto__` neutrality (no prototype pollution), credential masking at depth, fail-closed getter behavior — all pass.
- `tsc --noEmit -p packages/core/tsconfig.json` clean.
- `biome check` clean on both files.
- Verified end-to-end: `bun run dev` serves the UI (200) with the fix loaded.

## Files

- `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.ts` (3 clone sites + doc comment)
- `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.test.ts` (new)39:T41b,Make CLI login polling explicitly use the primary connection for active-session eligibility, preserving expiry checks and single-use credential retrieval. Both `dbRead` and `dbWrite` currently resolve to the primary in `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/client.ts`; this change makes the read-after-write contract explicit if read routing is separated later. The regression deliberately models a lagging replica and does not establish a current production replica-lag failure.

A route-level regression runs the real Hono handler, service and repository against two independent PGlite databases. It covers concurrent first polls, stale pending state after completion, consumed-key handling, expiry and malformed/unknown IDs. Unchanged code reproduces the erroneous 404; the fix passes all 16 assertions.

Validation: owning Cloud shared lint/typecheck and Cloud API typecheck/router/Worker dry-run passed. Full `bun run verify` passed (284/284 tasks and repository postchecks). Node 24.15.0 and Bun 1.4.2; source hashes were unchanged across verification.

3b:T1f5e,# Relates to

#32907 added funding-stop retention (#22967, owner decision #22957). A lapsed Plus/Pro plan can lose its retention clock on the first reconcile pass. That was the second blocking point of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32907#pullrequestreview-5345258500), and it is unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`.

In `agent-funding-retention.ts`:

- `discoverPlanLapses` (`:229-280`) creates a `subscription_lapsed` clock for any fallback in `fallback_pending` or `shared_active` with a `retain_until`. It never looks at the runtime.
- The fallback gets `retain_until` when it is created (`personal-dedicated-fallback.ts:340-357`).
- `activateInTransaction` (`personal-dedicated-fallback.ts:434-490`) commits `shared_active` in the same transaction that only **enqueues** the Dedicated suspend. On a lifecycle-admission conflict it leaves the fallback in `fallback_pending`. Either way the runtime is still `running` (or `provisioning`) when discovery first sees the lapse.
- `closeReason` (`:443-452`) returns `funding_restored` only when the fallback has left the withdrawn states. Otherwise it falls through to the agent-status check (`:473-478`), which returns `funding_restored` for a running agent, and for a provisioning one while the clock is `retained`.
- `reconcile` advances every open clock right after discovery, so the new clock is closed in the same pass.
- Discovery left-joins `agent_funding_retentions` on `fallback_id` (and migration 0501 has a unique index on it), so a fallback that already has a row, even a closed one, is never discovered again.

**Result:** that lapse gets no 7-day or 1-day notice and no deletion at the deadline. Its Dedicated container is retained indefinitely at no charge, which contradicts the 30-day policy.

# Change

`closeReason`: for a `subscription_lapsed` clock, the fallback interval decides.

- It returns `funding_restored` when the fallback is missing or has left `fallback_pending` / `shared_active`, as before.
- While the fallback is still withdrawn it now returns `null` (keep open) instead of falling through to the runtime status.
- `credits_exhausted` clocks are unchanged.

Deletion is unchanged too. It still requires a `stopped` runtime with no lifecycle job and no active resume (`:400-401`), so a runtime that is still shutting down only receives the notices, which is correct because the plan has lapsed.

The file header now states the two close conditions separately.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`4573712ebf`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

The new cases are in `agent-funding-retention.pglite.test.ts`. They use the same PGlite database, real migrations (0480/0490/0501), real `ProvisioningJobService` and fake clock as the existing retention tests. There are two cases:
- `shared_active` fallback with the runtime `running`;
- `fallback_pending` fallback with the runtime `provisioning`.

Each case:
1. The first reconcile discovers the lapse and must leave the clock `retained`. **On develop it reports `{ discovered: 1, closed: 1 }`.**
2. The suspend lands (runtime `stopped`) while the plan is still withdrawn, and the clock stays `retained`.
3. The fallback moves to `recovery_pending`, and only then does the clock close as `funding_restored`.

## Known gaps / failures

- `bun run verify` was not run in full on this host.
- Clocks already closed as `funding_restored` by this path on a fallback that is still withdrawn stay closed. Discovery cannot recreate them because of the `fallback_id` unique index from 0501.
  - An operator query finds them: closed `subscription_lapsed` rows whose fallback is still `fallback_pending` / `shared_active`.
  - Reopening them needs an owner decision on the deadline, so it is not automated here.
- A fallback that leaves `recovery_pending` for `fallback_pending` again (withdrawn during recovery) reuses the same interval. Its earlier clock was closed at recovery and is not reopened. That behavior is unchanged and is a separate policy question.

<h2>Version 6.18.0, 2026-09-07</h2>
<h2>What's new</h2>
<p>Please note that this release requires users which previously overwrote the default limits to migrate to the new approach: <a href="https://pypdf.readthedocs.io/en/6.18.0/user/security.html#global">Docs</a></p>
<p>In short:</p>
<ul>
<li>Use <code>apply_configuration</code> as a context manager to temporarily overwrite configuration values.</li>

You can trigger Dependabot actions by commenting on this PR:
- `@dependabot rebase` will rebase this PR
- `@dependabot recreate` will recreate this PR, overwriting any edits that have been made to it
- `@dependabot show <dependency name> ignore conditions` will show all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> major version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's major version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's major version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> minor version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's minor version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's minor version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name>` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency (unless you unignore this specific dependency or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name>` will remove all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name> <ignore condition>` will remove the ignore condition of the specified dependency and ignore conditions
You can disable automated security fix PRs for this repo from the [Security Alerts page](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/network/alerts).

</details>3d:T1c9c,# Relates to

#32926 (expose remote local-model readiness accurately) merged with the readiness probe gated on "`eliza-local-inference` is the sole text provider". The real plugin never satisfies that gate. This was the blocking point of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32926#pullrequestreview-5346555225); the follow-up commit reworked the availability check but kept the gate, and it is unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`.

- `readLocalTextModelReadiness` (`packages/agent/src/api/health-routes.ts:494-501`) returns `null` as soon as any text registration has a provider other than `eliza-local-inference`.
- The local-inference plugin registers its text handlers and then always installs its prefer-local router:
  - `installLocalRouterAndMark` → `installRouterHandler` (`ensure-local-inference-handler.ts:1597-1605`, called at `:1773`, `:1796` and `:1980`);
  - that registers `eliza-router` on each text slot at `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` (`router-handler.ts:94`, `:711-721`).
- `runtime.getModelRegistrations()` includes the router, so every real host looks "mixed". The probe returns `null`, and `computeCanRespond` never sees `model_not_loaded`.
- The HTTP fixture in `local-model-readiness-http.test.ts` registers only the bare `eliza-local-inference` handler, so the tests never hit this.

**Result:** on a desktop host with no local text model assigned or loaded, `/api/status` and the WebSocket status report `canRespond: true` and `localModelReadiness: null`. The composer goes live, and the first turn is refused by the router's own pre-model hook ("No local text model is assigned or loaded.", `router-handler.ts:665-707`).

# Change

`readLocalTextModelReadiness` ignores `eliza-router` registrations.

- The router serves nothing itself; it dispatches to the other registered handlers.
- A runtime whose only real text provider is `eliza-local-inference` now reports `available` / `model_not_loaded` as #32926 intended.
- Any real remote provider behind the router (`openai`, `elizaOSCloud`, a custom provider, and so on) still makes the probe return `null`, so mixed hosts keep their current behavior.

`isCloudTextModelUnavailable` is unchanged. The router is only installed together with a local or device text handler, which is already a non-Cloud failover for that type, so ignoring the router there would not change its answer.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`4573712ebf`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

`packages/agent/test/local-model-readiness-http.test.ts` (vitest, real `AgentRuntime` and authenticated HTTP/WebSocket server):

- The shared fixture now also registers `eliza-router` on `TEXT_SMALL` / `TEXT_LARGE` at `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`, which is what `installRouterHandler` does. The existing HTTP load/unload test and the WebSocket status test therefore run against the real registration shape.
- New: *treats the local router as part of the local provider, not as a second provider.*
  - Router plus local handler: `model_not_loaded` and `canRespond: false`.
  - Adding an `openai` handler: readiness `null` and `canRespond: true`.

| Same file, this head | **8 pass, 0 fail** |
| `packages/agent` `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` | pass |
| `biome check` on both files | clean |

## Known gaps / failures

- `bun run verify` was not run in full on this host.
- Mobile hosts register their text handlers under `eliza-aosp-llama` / `capacitor-llama` (and `eliza-device-bridge` for a paired device). The probe still reports `null` (unknown) there, as before. Mapping those providers onto the UI's `eliza-local-inference` readiness contract is a separate change.
- As #32926's review noted, turning the gate on means a desktop host with no local model assigned or loaded now reports `canRespond: false`. That matches the router's own refusal for that turn, but it has not been exercised on a physical desktop build here.

3f:Tcfa,# Relates to

Formatted `Thought:` text in a labeled evaluator verdict was promoted to `messageToUser` when an explicit reply was absent. This could replace a terminal planner answer with internal commentary; it was encountered while validating native onboarding replies.

# Change

Keep labeled evaluator thoughts as diagnostic fields. Preserve explicit replies and let the existing terminal-planner path supply the user answer. No renderer text filtering, prompt changes, tool replay or vendor repin.

</details>42:T1ae2,# Relates to

Develop's `canonical / Plugin Tests (3/4)` lane has been red since #32991 landed: at head `3f38e54495ba5518f84bcf9cc1e84e0dc3d60bbf`, `canonical / All Tests Passed` fails with `tests_plugins finished with failure`, and the failing test is `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/work-thread-native-schema.test.ts > WORK_THREAD native arguments (Cerebras: false) > exposes supported operations and preserves sparse lifecycle arguments` (`AssertionError: expected false to be true` at `work-thread-native-schema.test.ts:99`).

# Change

`validateToolArgs` is the admission boundary between model-produced tool arguments and action handlers. #32991 made it validate an explicit `null` against the declared child schema so that schemas which declare nullability (`type: "null"`, nullable enum, `anyOf` null branch) keep the model's explicit null. That preserved intent remains intact.

But strict native-tool grammars require every property, so on the non-Cerebras strict-tool wire an omitted optional argument arrives as explicit `null` — the encoding `plugins/plugin-openai` restores from provider tool calls and `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/work-thread-native-schema.test.ts` pins as a live observed contract ("non-Cerebras strict tools also represent omitted optionals as null"). After #32991, every such restored call failed admission (`workThreadId: null` against `type: "string"` → type error), so WORK_THREAD — and any action with optional parameters called through that path — got rejected before handler execution.

This reconciles both contracts at the same single admission boundary:

- a schema that admits `null` keeps the explicit null (unchanged from #32991);
- a `null` that fails an **optional** property's schema is the wire's absence encoding and resolves like an omitted key (declared default applied, key dropped);
- a `null` on a **required** property still fails through the child-schema type error.

Material risk: a model that emits a spurious `null` for an optional property is now treated as "omitted" rather than rejected. That was the admission behavior on develop before #32991 (and `resolve-action-args.ts`'s `valueIsPresent` already treats `null` as not-present), and it is what the pinned live wire contract requires; strict tools cannot express optional absence any other way.

# Testing

All commands run from a clean shallow checkout of `develop` at `3f38e5449` with the pinned Node `v24.15.0`:

1. **Reproduction (before this commit):** `bunx vitest run --config plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/vitest.config.ts plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/work-thread-native-schema.test.ts` → `1 failed | 1 passed` — identical to the red CI lane (`AssertionError: expected false to be true` at line 99).
2. **After this commit, same command:** `2 passed`.
3. **Core suite:** `bun run --cwd packages/core test` → `Test Files 82 passed | 2 skipped (84)`, `Tests 1091 passed | 2 skipped (1093)` — includes the updated `nullable-schema.integration.test.ts` and every pre-existing admission test.
4. **Typecheck:** `bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck` → clean.
5. **Lint/format:** `bunx biome check` on both changed files → clean.

`bun run verify` was not fully green locally for unrelated environment reasons: local Bun is `1.4.0` while the repo pins `1.4.2`, the SQLite driver runtime guard rejects `1.4.0`, and the postinstall workspace build fails in `plugins/plugin-elizacloud` package verification (`dist/lib/config-env` missing) on vanilla develop before this change. PR CI runs the pinned toolchain and owns the full gate.

43:T1c64,# Relates to

#32910 (subscriptions + pay-as-you-go pricing policy) added the Cloud-credit refund path for crypto and x402 payments (#22968). For an x402 **payment request**, that path credits the payee instead of the payer. This was the blocking point of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32910#pullrequestreview-5345370970). The later `status === "confirmed"` guard (#32915) did not change the recipient, so it is unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`.

- `x402PaymentRequestsService.create` stores the `crypto_payments` row under `organization_id: input.organizationId, user_id: input.userId` (`x402-payment-requests.ts:650-651`). The route passes the **requesting** user's organization (`api/v1/x402/requests/route.ts:65-66`), so the row belongs to the payee.
- On settlement that creator is paid, through `recordAppScopedPaymentEarnings` or `redeemableEarningsService.addEarnings({ userId: payment.user_id, source: "creator_revenue_share" })` (`x402-payment-requests.ts:809-814`). The payer is only recorded as a wallet in `metadata.payer` (`:800`).
- `refundAsCloudCredits` treats every `metadata.kind === "x402_payment_request"` row as the x402 rail (`crypto-payment-refunds.ts:81`). It requires `payment.organization_id === input.organizationId` (`:143-149`) and credits that organization (`:203`).
- x402 top-ups never create `crypto_payments` rows (`topup-handler.ts` writes the ledger directly), so every x402 row this service can reach is a payment request.

**Result:** an operator refund of an x402 payment request gives the payee Cloud credits on top of the earnings they already received, and the payer gets nothing. The existing test pinned this behavior: *"x402 payments refund as credits to the owning organization only"* refunds the x402 row to its owner org and expects success.

# Change

`refundAsCloudCredits` refuses the x402 rail with a new typed error, `CRYPTO_REFUND_X402_PAYER_UNBOUND`.

- The check runs after the destination-policy check, so on-chain and fiat requests keep their existing error, and before any refund-state read or ledger write.
- Crypto (wallet and OxaPay) refunds are unchanged.
- `CRYPTO_REFUND_POLICY` and the top-up refund statement are unchanged. Refunds are still credits-only; x402 payment requests simply have no organization to credit yet.
- The service header and the operator script's doc describe the refusal.
- The now-unreachable x402 branch of the refund description is removed.

This is deliberately fail-closed. Refunding an x402 payer properly needs a payer-to-organization binding and a reversal of the creator's earnings in the same transaction. That is a product decision under #22968, not something this PR invents.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`4573712ebf`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

`crypto-payment-refunds.pglite.test.ts` exercises the real refund service and credit ledger on PGlite. The old x402 test is replaced by *x402 payment requests are refused: the record belongs to the payee, not the payer*. For both the owning (payee) org and another org, the refund rejects with `CRYPTO_REFUND_X402_PAYER_UNBOUND`, no `credit_transactions` row is written, and the payee's balance stays 0.

<h2>effect@4.0.0-rc.116</h2>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/Effect-TS/effect/pull/7908">#7908</a> <a href="https://github.com/Effect-TS/effect/commit/c19c63fb710422aaf00b8d923188aa2e52a6776f"><code>c19c63f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/gcanti"><code>@​gcanti</code></a>! - Add experimental JIT and AOT schema compilers that work through the existing
<code>SchemaParser</code> APIs and share a decoder registry. Enable JIT globally with
<code>effect/unstable/schema/SchemaJITCompiler/enable</code>, selectively with
<code>SchemaJITCompiler.enable(ast)</code>, or install generated AOT decoders without
requiring dynamic function construction. The new
<code>effect/unstable/schema/SchemaAOTCompiler/Build</code> entrypoint discovers direct
Schema exports from explicit module loaders and writes a self-installing AOT
module through Effect's <code>FileSystem</code> and <code>Path</code> services. Compiled decoders can
provide optional synchronous <code>decode</code> and <code>make</code> operations; normal
<code>SchemaParser</code> calls consume them transparently and retain <code>decodeEffect</code> and
<code>makeEffect</code> as the detailed fallbacks. AOT targets declare the operations to
prepare, so generated modules contain only those operation families and use</li>
</ul>
